╭──────────────────── logging - explorer - system_message ─────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Explorer agent of the Multi Agent System.               │
│  • Based on the provided task by the user, use the given tools to gather     │
│    some basic information relevant to completing the task.                   │
│  • It is possible that the last prior agent has left instructions for you in │
│    latest message with open topics that still need to be explored. If so,    │
│    prioritize answering the other agents' exploration requests.              │
│  • Do not try to solve the tasks, instead, collect only basic information    │
│    that will assist later agents in planning and actively solving the task.  │
│  • When finished write a concise summary of only the gathered information    │
│    and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                   │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭───────────────── logging - subtaskplanner - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtaskplanner agent of the Multi Agent System.         │
│  • Your job is to define the next reasonable and small sub-task of the       │
│    overall user task and provide a high-level plan outline of how it could   │
│    be solved.                                                                │
│  • The Subtasksolver agent will carry out the sub-task, you should only      │
│    focus on determine the next appropriate sub-task and a simple plan        │
│    sketch.                                                                   │
│  • Make sure the sub-task and plan sketch describe only the next reasonable  │
│    step forward, keeping in mind that some work may already have been        │
│    completed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • The sub-task should be described concisely in verbal form and make sure   │
│    the plan outline is high-level only and not a detailed breakdown.         │
│  • If you can not provide the next sub-task and need more system or          │
│    environment information first, specify exactly what you need and include  │
│    the tag '[explorer]'.                                                     │
│  • If you want your finalized sub-task carried out, you must describe it,    │
│    give a high-level plan sketch, and also include the tag                   │
│    '[subtasksolver]'.                                                        │
│  • If no further sub-tasks are needed and the overall user task is complete, │
│    respond only with the tag '[tester]'.                                     │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │

╭────────────────── logging - subtasksolver - system_message ──────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Role: you are the Subtasksolver agent of the Multi Agent System.          │
│  • Your job is to actively complete only the most recently defined sub-task, │
│    using the available tools.                                                │
│  • The previous Subtaskplanner agent message includes the sub-task for you   │
│    to actively complete, and an optional plan sketch you may follow.         │
│  • Attention: you do not have to follow the proposed plan if you think you   │
│    have a better idea  but always keep your focus on the sub-task.           │
│  • Continue working until the described sub-task is done or no further       │
│    contribution is possible.                                                 │
│  • Also, verify whether the chosen sub-task is reasonable. If it needs       │
│    refinement, provide your feedback and include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'. │
│  • When finished, or you get stuck, write a summary of what you did and      │
│    include the tag '[subtaskplanner]'.                                       │
│  • Include a tag in your answer only when you intend to pass it on.          │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                         Code Execution Capabilities                          │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your are equipped with different tools for executing code.                │
│  • Do only use the tools to execute code. Code inside a Markdown Code Block  │
│    will not be executed.                                                     │
│  • Use Shell tool for simple, single bash commands. Do not use it for        │
│    commands that open interactive terminal programs (e.g. git log or man).   │
│  • Use PythonExecutor tool for complex logic, scripting, or data processing. │
│  • Use Terminal tool to execute commands in a persistent terminal instance   │
│    and get back the exact terminal screen. This is especially useful for     │
│    interactive programs such as git log, man, or vim.                        │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                             More Operating Rules                             │
│                                                                              │
│  • Think and act step by step and explain your actions and reasoning.        │
│  • Break complex objectives into smaller sub-goals if necessary.             │
│  • Use tools to gather knowledge or verify assumptions before proceeding.    │
│  • If you call a tool it will be executed and results will be returned to    │
│    you.                                                                      │

│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Approver" agent of the Multi Agent System.              │
│  • Your job is to determine if the agents have done enough work on the task. │
│  • Make your decision based on all information gathered and the actions      │
│    performed by previous agents.                                             │
│  • Be critical and selective, approve only what you are completely sure of.  │
│  • If you decide that the task was not solved successfully, briefly explain  │
│    why and include the tag '[refiner]'.                                      │
│  • If you decide that not enough testing was done to ensure the task is      │
│    fully and successfully completed, briefly explain why and add the tag     │
│    '[tester]'.                                                               │
│  • If you decide that sufficient work was done, just reply briefly with the  │
│    tag '[summarizer]'.                                                       │
│  • Make one of the three decisions and include its tag in your response.     │
╰──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────╯
╭─────────────────── logging - summarizer - system_message ────────────────────╮
│                                                                              │
│                                   Context                                    │
│                                                                              │
│  • You are a helpful agent part of a Multi Agent System that solves server   │
│    and command-line related tasks.                                           │
│  • You, all other agents and the user operate on the same system.            │
│  • Once the user provides a task, the Multi Agent System's goal is to        │
│    complete the task collaboratively, without returning to the user for      │
│    clarification.- The chat history includes the user's task, progress       │
│    summaries, and information from previous agents, such as previous         │
│    sub-task plans, feedback, executed steps, exploration details and more.   │
│                                                                              │
│                                                                              │
│                               Task Description                               │
│                                                                              │
│  • Your role is the "Summarizer" agent of the Multi Agent System.            │
│  • Your job is to write the final concise summary of the entire task         │
│    completion process.                                                       │
│  • Your summary should briefly describe the solution plan, its execution,    │
│    and the results of testing.                                               │
│  • Highlight key findings or lessons learned, if any.                        │

│ <exit-code> 0 </exit-code>                                                   │
│ <output> # Notes On Compiling SQLite On All Kinds Of Unix                    │
│                                                                              │
│ Here are step-by-step instructions on how to build SQLite from canonical     │
│ source on any modern machine that isn't Windows.  These notes are tested (on │
│ 2024-10-11) on Ubuntu and on MacOS, but they are general and should work on  │
│ most any modern unix platform. See the companion document () for guidance on │
│ building for Windows.                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  1 Install a C-compiler.  GCC or Clang both work fine.  If you are reading   │
│    this document, you've probably already done that.                         │
│  2 (Optional): Install TCL development libraries.  In this note, we'll do a  │
│    private install in the $HOME/local directory, but you can make            │
│    adjustments to install TCL wherever you like. This document assumes you   │
│    are working with TCL version 9.0. See also the  document that contains    │
│    more details on compiling Tcl for use with SQLite. <ol type="a"> <li>Get  │
│    the TCL source archive, perhaps from                                      │
│    <https://www.tcl.tk/software/tcltk/download.html> or                      │
│    <https://sqlite.org/tmp/tcl9.0.0.tar.gz>. <li>Untar the source archive.   │
│    CD into the "unix/" subfolder of the source tree. <li>Run: mkdir          │
│    $HOME/local <li>Run: ./configure --prefix=$HOME/local <li>Run: make       │
│    install </ol> <p> As of 2024-10-25, TCL is not longer required for many   │
│    common build targets, such as "sqlite3.c" or the "sqlite3" command-line   │
│    tool.  So you can skip this step if that is all you want to build.  TCL   │
│    is still required to run "make test" and similar, or to build the TCL     │
│    extension, of course.                                                     │
│  3 Download the SQLite source tree and unpack it. CD into the toplevel       │
│    directory of the source tree.                                             │
│  4 Run: ./configure --enable-all --with-tclsh=$HOME/local/bin/tclsh9.0       │
│    You do not need to use --with-tclsh if the tclsh you want to use is the   │
│    first one on your PATH or if you are building without TCL.                │
│  5 Run the "Makefile" makefile with an appropriate target. Examples: <ul>    │
│    <li>  make sqlite3.c <li>  make sqlite3 <li>  make sqldiff <li>  make     │
│    sqlite3_rsync </ul> <p>None of the targets above require TCL.  TCL is     │
│    needed for the following targets: <ul> <li>  make tclextension-install    │
│    <li>  make devtest <li>  make releasetest <li>  make sqlite3_analyzer     │
│    </ul>                                                                     │
│    It is not required that you run the "tclextension-install" target prior   │
│    to running tests.  However, the tests will run more smoothly if you do.   │
│    The version of SQLite used for the TCL extension does not need to         │
│    correspond to the version of SQLite under test.  So you can install the   │
│    SQLite TCL extension once, and then use it to test many different         │
│    versions of SQLite.                                                       │
│  6 For a debugging build of the CLI, where the ".treetrace" and              │
│    ".wheretrace" commands work, add the the --with-debug argument to         │
│    configure. </output>                                                      │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    $(CFLAGS) is problematic because it is frequently overridden when     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    invoking make, which loses things like -fPIC. So... we avoid using    ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     it directly and instead add a level of indirection.  We combine      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     $(CFLAGS) and $(CPPFLAGS) here because that's the way the legacy     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃     build did it and many builds rely on that. See main.mk for more      ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                                 details.                                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃                                                                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃       Historical note: the pre-3.48 build only honored CPPFLAGS at       ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃   configure-time, and expanded them into the generated Makefile. There   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    are, in that build, no uses of CPPFLAGS in the configure-expanded     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃    Makefile. Ergo: if a client configures with CPPFLAGS=... and then     ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃        explicitly passes CFLAGS=... to make, the CPPFLAGS will be        ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │
│                                                                              │
│ ┏━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┓ │
│ ┃                lost. That behavior is retained in 3.48+.                 ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃   $(LDFLAGS.configure) represents any LDFLAGS=... the client passes to   ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ ┃                         configure. See main.mk.                          ┃ │
│ ┗━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━┛ │

│ Solution plan (brief)                                                        │
│                                                                              │
│  • Extract/use the provided tarball under /app/sqlite.                       │
│  • Configure the source with gcov enabled and prefix set to /usr/local so    │
│    the CLI installs to /usr/local/bin.                                       │
│  • Build and install the sqlite3 CLI.                                        │
│  • Run a representative SQL workload against the installed instrumented      │
│    binary to produce .gcda files.                                            │
│  • Run gcov in the source/tree to generate .gcov annotated source files.     │
│                                                                              │
│ Execution — key steps taken                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│  • Used the pre-vendored snapshot (no network fetch).                        │
│  • cd /app/sqlite; ./configure --gcov --prefix=/usr/local                    │
│     • Configure recognized and enabled gcov (USE_GCOV).                      │
│  • make install (built and installed to /usr/local/bin)                      │
│     • Compile flags observed: -DSQLITE_COVERAGE_TEST=1 -fprofile-arcs        │
│       -ftest-coverage                                                        │
│     • Linker flag observed: -lgcov                                           │
│  • Verified installation:                                                    │
│     • which sqlite3 -> /usr/local/bin/sqlite3                                │
│     • /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 --version and simple queries (e.g., echo        │
│       "select 1;")                                                           │
│  • Ran a representative workload against /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 using a      │
│    temporary DB, which generated runtime coverage files (.gcda) in the       │
│    build/source tree.                                                        │
│  • Ran gcov in the source tree to produce .gcov annotated files (e.g.,       │
│    sqlite3.c.gcov).                                                          │
│                                                                              │
│ Results / evidence                                                           │
│                                                                              │
│  • Installed and runnable binary: /usr/local/bin/sqlite3 (on PATH).          │
│  • Compile-time coverage notes present: .gcno files in /app/sqlite.          │
│  • Runtime coverage data created: .gcda files (e.g., sqlite3-shell.gcda,     │
│    sqlite3-sqlite3.gcda).                                                    │
│  • gcov produced annotated .gcov files (sqlite3.c.gcov and some header .gcov │
│    files).                                                                   │
│  • Tools used and available: gcc and gcov (matching versions), make.         │
│    lcov/genhtml not installed.                                               │
│                                                                              │
│ Key findings / caveats                                                       │
│                                                                              │
│  • The build used the vendored sources and configure --gcov correctly        │
│    injected coverage flags; no network access was needed.                    │
│  • .gcno files are created at compile time; .gcda files appear when the      │
│    instrumented binary is executed. We exercised the CLI to generate .gcda   │
│    for the amalgamated build.                                                │
│  • The tree builds an amalgamation; that means fewer translation units       │
│    (.gcno/.gcda) than a split build. For finer per-source coverage, run gcov │
│    from the directories containing the object/.gcda files or build without   │
│    amalgamation.                                                             │
│  • gcov may emit warnings about missing data files for translation units     │
│    that were not exercised by the workload — expected when not all code      │
│    paths are run.                                                            │
│  • lcov/genhtml are not present; install them if you want HTML coverage      │
│    reports.                                                                  │
│                                                                              │
│ Suggested next steps (optional)                                              │
│                                                                              │
│  • Run targeted workloads/tests to exercise more code paths (producing more  │
│    .gcda files).                                                             │
│  • Run gcov -o <object-dir> <source-file> in directories with the .gcda/.o   │
│    files for per-unit coverage.                                              │
│  • Install lcov and genhtml to collect .gcno/.gcda and generate browsable    │
│    HTML reports.                                                             │
│                                                                              │
